*** Objective and working rules

Implement all three tools in one working session:

- ~web_fetch~
- ~delete_file~
- ~apply_patch~, supporting several files in one call

Finish the implementation, tests, documentation, and integration. Do not stop after scaffolding or implementing only one tool.

Write idiomatic, readable, efficient Emacs Lisp. Prefer small, cohesive functions and explicit data structures. Avoid speculative abstractions, unnecessary dependencies, unrelated refactoring, and compatibility aliases nobody needs.

Tests should establish useful behavioral guarantees, not mirror implementation details. Use representative scenarios and table-driven boundary tests rather than dozens of narrowly tailored tests.

Commit coherent, tested steps as you go. Use short, human-readable commit subjects. Do not commit unrelated working-tree changes, generated files, or temporary artifacts.

*** 1. Inspect the repository and establish a baseline

Before editing:

1. Read repository instructions and relevant documentation.
2. Inspect the working tree and preserve existing user changes.
3. Run ~make check~ and record any pre-existing failures.
4. Inspect the implementation and callers of:
   - ~ende-tool-register~ and ~ende-tool-call~
   - ~ende-tools--path-authorized-p~
   - ~ende-change-set~ and the existing create/edit handlers
   - Resource parsing, inheritance, and grants in ~ende-chat-config.el~
   - ~ende-project-path~ and visiting-buffer discovery
   - Tool dispatch and cancellation in ~ende-chat.el~ and ~ende-queue.el~
   - Curl process management in ~ende-curl.el~
   - The dependency order in ~ende-reload~

Relevant existing tests include:

- ~tests/ende-tools-test.el~
- ~tests/ende-chat-config-test.el~
- ~tests/ende-chat-tools-test.el~
- ~tests/ende-transport-test.el~

Important existing behavior to preserve:

- Untyped resources describe project-relative file patterns.
- Configuration is parsed as data, never evaluated.
- Resource and group redefinitions revoke inherited grants according to the existing rules.
- Tool activation and resource assignment are separate.
- Existing file tools operate on disk, not visiting-buffer contents.
- ~edit_file~ currently permits dirty visiting buffers, leaves them untouched, and returns warnings. Existing tests explicitly cover this.

Do not silently change that last behavior while sharing implementation with the new tools. The new deletion and patch tools will use a stricter dirty-buffer policy.

*** 2. Add typed resources and separate project-root requirements

Suggested commit: ~Add typed tool resources~

**** Registry changes

Represent these requirements independently:

- Resource kind: ~file~, ~url~, or none.
- Whether a project root is required.
- Whether the tool mutates local files.

Use these classifications:

| Tool category                  | Resource kind | Requires root | Mutates files |
|--------------------------------+---------------+---------------+---------------|
| Existing file readers          | file          | yes           | no            |
| Existing create/edit tools     | file          | yes           | yes           |
| ~delete_file~, ~apply_patch~    | file          | yes           | yes           |
| ~project_info~                 | none          | yes           | no            |
| ~web_fetch~                    | url           | no            | no            |

“No local file mutation” does not mean fetching is side-effect-free: it sends a network request.

Keep existing registration calls working. Update consumers of the current ~requires-resource~ flag consistently; avoid maintaining two contradictory sources of truth.

**** Resource syntax

Support:

#+begin_src lisp
(resource source
  (include "src/**")
  (exclude "src/generated/**"))

(resource source-explicit :type file
  (include "src/**"))

(resource documentation :type url
  (include "https://example.com/**")
  (include "https://*.example.com/**")
  (exclude "https://private.example.com/**"))
#+end_src

Requirements:

- Omitted ~:type~ means ~file~.
- Preserve existing file-pattern behavior exactly. Do not reinterpret existing patterns as gitignore-style patterns.
- Reject unsupported resource types, malformed options, and incompatible tool/resource assignments.
- Preserve existing assignments to resource-free tools within file-tool groups.
- Validate assignments to inactive tools too: those grants can authorize operations inside a patch.
- Every resource still requires an explicit include.
- Preserve inheritance, replacement/addition of grants, and grant revocation on resource redefinition.

Resource composition remains:

1. Within a resource: at least one include matches and no exclude matches.
2. Across assigned resources: permission is the union of those resources.

An exclusion in one resource does not override an allowance in another. Document this; do not introduce a new global deny-list language.

**** Root handling

Require an explicit configured root only when an active tool requires one.

A web-only chat must work without a project, including when its current buffer is outside any project. Avoid unconditional project discovery or root normalization in dispatch.

An explicitly supplied invalid root should still produce an error.

Keep model-originated calls bound to the frozen chat policy. Preserve existing trusted, no-policy Elisp use of file tools, but require an explicit URL policy for ~web_fetch~. There must be no unrestricted-fetch fallback.

**** URL policy implementation

Put URL parsing and policy matching in a small module, such as ~ende-url.el~, shared by configuration and fetching. Do not reuse the file wildcard matcher against complete URLs.

Implement these fixed semantics:

- Bare DNS hostnames are shorthand for HTTPS and all paths:
  - ~example.com~ means ~https://example.com/**~.
  - ~*.example.com~ means ~https://*.example.com/**~.
- Patterns containing paths or ports must be absolute HTTP(S) patterns.
- Exact hosts match exactly. Never automatically add or remove ~www~.
- ~*.example.com~ matches one or more subdomain labels, but not the apex or ~evil-example.com~.
- A whole-host ~*~ may match any DNS hostname. IP literals still require explicit literal-host rules.
- HTTP and HTTPS are separate permissions.
- An omitted port means the scheme’s default port.
- Path matching is case-sensitive. ~*~ does not cross a slash; ~**~ can.
- Queries are allowed in request URLs but are not filtered by resource patterns. Reject query/fragment syntax in patterns.
- Strip request fragments before authorization and transmission.
- Support exact IPv4 and bracketed IPv6 hosts. No IP globs or CIDR syntax in this version.

Use one canonicalization path for both policy patterns and requests. Normalize hostname case, a terminal DNS dot, default ports, and path dot segments consistently. Support ASCII/punycode hostnames initially; reject raw Unicode hostnames with an actionable message.

Reject user information, control characters, backslashes, malformed escapes, legacy numeric IPv4 spellings, and IPv6 zone identifiers. Normalize unreserved percent escapes before path matching; reject encoded path separators and nested escaping rather than guessing how another parser will interpret them.

Authorize the normalized URL that will actually be transmitted. Document that path filters constrain outgoing URLs, not how a remote application routes or interprets them.

Add policy and inheritance tests in this commit, including a rootless URL-tool fixture without exposing an unfinished production tool.

*** 3. Share file mutation preparation and commit logic

Suggested commit: ~Separate file preparation from writes~

Build a small internal mutation pipeline around the existing change-set machinery. Keep it in ~ende-tools.el~ unless a separate cohesive module is clearly warranted. Do not build a general transaction framework.

Separate:

1. Resolving and authorizing an operation.
2. Reading its source and preparing its result.
3. Validating the complete batch.
4. Committing changes.
5. Recovering from a failed commit.

Refactor existing ~create_file~ and ~edit_file~ to use the shared pieces without changing their schemas, normal behavior, or result fields.

**** Preparation

For a batch:

- Enumerate all operations and paths first.
- Authorize all operations before reading target contents or staging changes.
- Detect duplicate canonical targets and conflicting operations on the same existing file.
- Record original contents, coding system, line-ending style, mode, identity, and relevant preconditions.
- Prepare all resulting contents before changing any target.
- Require existing parent directories, matching current ~create_file~ behavior.
- Apply reasonable limits to patch size, operation count, and cumulative prepared data.

Keep the public digest semantics compatible with ~read_file~ and ~file_info~. For internal race detection and recovery, also retain enough raw-byte and file-identity information to detect changes that a decoded-text digest would miss.

**** Commit and recovery

Immediately before committing, recheck the complete batch. Recheck each target again before its individual mutation.

- Revalidate containment, permissions, existence/nonexistence, identity, contents, and applicable dirty-buffer rules.
- Use exclusive creation for new files. Never overwrite a file that appeared after preparation.
- Preserve existing coding systems, line endings, and file modes.
- Keep private recovery copies until the batch has completed.
- Use restrictive permissions for temporary and recovery data.
- Prevent overlapping Ende commits from interleaving on the same project.
- Clean up owned resources with ~unwind-protect~.

If a commit fails:

- Attempt rollback in reverse order.
- Do not overwrite a concurrent user change while rolling back.
- Retain recovery material when automatic restoration is unsafe or unsuccessful.
- Return an honest per-file account of what was applied, restored, or still needs recovery.

Do not claim crash-safe, multi-file atomicity or a race-free filesystem sandbox. Portable file operations do not provide those guarantees.

The contract is:

#+begin_quote
A preflight permission, syntax, context, or precondition failure leaves every target file and visiting buffer unchanged.

A failure after committing starts triggers best-effort rollback and an explicit recovery report.
#+end_quote

Extend the dispatcher narrowly so structured mutation failures retain their details. Preserve the existing readable ~:error~ string and use ~:ok nil~ for failed or partial batches. Do not let the generic error handler discard the recovery report.

Never modify, save, revert, or kill visiting buffers as part of these operations.

*** 4. Implement file deletion

Suggested commit: ~Add safe file deletion~

Register:

#+begin_src text
delete_file(path, expected_digest?)
#+end_src

Contract:

- Delete one existing regular file.
- No directories, recursive deletion, or wildcard expansion.
- Require the ~delete_file~ resource grant independently of read/edit permissions.
- Reject final-component symlinks, including dangling symlinks.
- Resolve parent directories and enforce the existing project boundary and global exclusions.
- Reject absolute paths, remote paths, invalid path characters, and parent-traversal components in this new API.
- Refuse deletion if any visiting buffer for the target has unsaved changes.
- Leave clean visiting buffers intact and return a warning that saving them could recreate the file.
- When supplied, ~expected_digest~ must match the digest exposed by the existing file-read/info tools.
- Missing files and stale digests are errors, not silent successes.

Check the final component for symlinks /before/ calling code that resolves it through ~file-truename~. Otherwise, deletion could remove the link’s target.

Honor ~delete-by-moving-to-trash~ explicitly. Never silently fall back to permanent deletion when a requested trash operation fails.

Use the shared mutation pipeline. Capture visiting-buffer information before the file disappears.

Return a normal JSON-compatible result identifying the operation, project-relative path, status, and relevant buffer warnings. Do not return deleted contents.

There is no model-controlled force option.

*** 5. Implement multi-file patching

Suggested commit: ~Add multi-file patches~

Register:

#+begin_src text
apply_patch(patch)
#+end_src

Use a documented, deliberately limited Codex-style text format. Put pure parsing and hunk application in ~ende-patch.el~; keep filesystem access and authorization out of the parser.

**** Supported format

Support:

- ~*** Begin Patch~
- ~*** Add File: PATH~
- ~*** Update File: PATH~
- ~*** Delete File: PATH~
- Bare ~@@~ hunk separators.
- Context lines beginning with a space, removed lines with ~-~, and added lines with ~+~.
- ~*** End of File~ to constrain an update hunk to EOF.
- ~*** End Patch~

Requirements:

- Parse the entire input before performing filesystem work.
- Require project-relative literal paths.
- Match context exactly and case-sensitively.
- Reject absent, ambiguous, overlapping, or out-of-order hunk matches.
- Require context for insertion-only hunks, except when updating an empty file.
- Reject multiple operation records targeting the same canonical file.
- Reject malformed input and unsupported constructs explicitly.

No fuzzy matching, shell invocation, external ~patch~ command, rename/move syntax, binary patches, mode changes, symlink creation, or implicit directory creation in this version.

Treat the protocol as logical lines. Preserve existing encoding, line endings, and final-newline style on updates. New files use UTF-8 with Unix line endings; nonempty added files end in a newline. Empty additions are supported.

Document this as a supported subset, not full compatibility with another tool’s patch language.

**** Authorization

Every operation must pass both:

1. The ~apply_patch~ resource scope.
2. The corresponding underlying operation scope:
   - Update: ~edit_file~.
   - Add: ~create_file~.
   - Delete: ~delete_file~.

Underlying standalone tools need not be active or exposed to the model. Their grants must exist and authorize the operation.

Use a shared authorization helper with an explicit operation/tool identity. Do not invoke ~ende-tool-call~ recursively or temporarily weaken the policy.

Consequences to document and test:

- A patch cannot delete merely because it can edit.
- Hiding ~delete_file~ does not revoke an existing delete grant.
- ~clear-tools~ controls exposure; grant removal controls operation authorization.

**** Batch behavior

Run the entire patch through the shared preparation/commit pipeline, not a loop over immediately mutating handlers.

For this new tool:

- Reject final-component symlinks.
- Refuse the entire patch if any affected visiting buffer is dirty.
- Apply the same deletion/trash rules as standalone deletion.
- Reject adding over any existing filesystem entry.
- Leave visiting buffers untouched and report stale-buffer warnings.
- Return per-file operation results as a real JSON array.
- Report recovery details on commit failure.

*** 6. Implement bounded, permission-aware web fetching

Suggested commit: ~Add bounded web fetching~

Register:

#+begin_src text
web_fetch(url, max_chars?)
#+end_src

Keep transport code in ~ende-web-fetch.el~, separate from provider authentication. Reuse neutral process/cleanup helpers where appropriate, but do not route fetching through provider-profile validation or credential construction.

**** Destination authorization

For the initial URL and every redirect:

1. Parse and normalize the URL.
2. Check its URL-resource permission before DNS lookup.
3. Resolve the destination.
4. Classify the returned addresses.
5. Connect only to a vetted address.

Use numeric address parsing and prefix checks, not textual prefix matching.

By default reject non-public destinations, including loopback, private, link-local, unspecified, multicast, reserved, and other special-use ranges. Handle IPv4-mapped IPv6 explicitly. Unsupported transition/address forms must fail closed rather than bypass classification.

Use a clearly documented address-classification table. Do not download policy data at runtime.

For local services, support this explicit resource option:

#+begin_src lisp
(resource local-service :type url :allow-private-addresses t
  (include "http://127.0.0.1:3000/**"))
#+end_src

Limit this opt-in to loopback, RFC1918 IPv4, and IPv6 unique-local addresses. Link-local metadata endpoints and other special-use destinations remain blocked.

Private-enabled resources must use exact hosts, not wildcard hosts. The exception applies only when that resource actually permits the requested URL; it must not become a global switch.

Require all resolved addresses to satisfy the applicable policy before selecting one. Pin the connection to the selected address while retaining the original hostname for HTTP Host, TLS SNI, and certificate verification. Curl’s ~--resolve~ is suitable for this.

Do not validate DNS and then let curl independently resolve the hostname again. Never fall back to an unvetted address.

**** Transport restrictions

Use curl through argument vectors, never through a shell.

- Disable curl configuration-file loading.
- Disable ambient proxies for this tool, regardless of the provider proxy option.
- Do not inherit provider headers, auth-source credentials, cookies, netrc configuration, or other authenticated client state.
- Keep TLS certificate verification enabled.
- Allow HTTP and HTTPS only.
- Disable curl URL globbing.
- Do not use automatic redirect following.
- Follow only supported HTTP redirects, resolving relative locations and reauthorizing each hop.
- Do not fetch scripts, stylesheets, images, or other subresources.

The model cannot supply methods, headers, credentials, proxy settings, or transport flags.

Use finite tool-specific defaults, for example:

- 10-second connection deadline.
- 30-second overall call deadline.
- Five redirects.
- 2 MiB cumulative response-body limit.
- 64 KiB cumulative response-header limit.
- 50,000 returned characters.

The overall deadline must cover resolution and redirect processing too. Do not assume a timer can interrupt a blocking resolver primitive; use a bounded resolver execution path.

Enforce byte limits during receipt, including responses without Content-Length and chunked responses. A post-download size check or curl’s filesize option alone is insufficient.

Request identity content encoding and reject unsupported encodings in this version rather than adding an unbounded decompression path.

A model-supplied ~max_chars~ may lower the configured output ceiling, never raise it.

**** Content and result behavior

Return bounded raw text/HTML; do not introduce browser rendering or HTML extraction.

Accept documented textual MIME types, including HTML, plain text, JSON, and XML. Reject binary or unsupported content types clearly.

Successful results should include:

- Requested and final URL.
- HTTP status.
- Content type.
- Content.
- Whether the returned text was truncated.

A completed textual HTTP error response can be returned with its HTTP status. DNS, TLS, transport, policy, timeout, and hard-size failures use ~:ok nil~.

Only deliberate output clipping sets ~truncated~. Do not disguise an interrupted transfer as a successful truncated response.

Fetched content remains untrusted tool data. Never evaluate it, activate embedded Ende configuration, or let it alter the frozen policy.

**** Cancellation and integration

Preserve the ordinary synchronous ~ende-tool-call~ result interface. Internally, use managed processes and a cancellable wait rather than an uninterruptible process call.

Connect chat cancellation to the active fetch. On cancellation:

- Stop resolver/HTTP processes.
- Release owned buffers, files, and timers.
- Do not execute later tool calls in that round.
- Do not start another model request.

Keep this integration small; do not redesign the whole scheduler.

Update module loading and ~ende-reload~ in dependency order. Avoid require cycles between tools, configuration, URL policy, and transport modules.

*** 7. Test behavior, not implementation shape

Add tests alongside each implementation step. Do not postpone all testing until the end.

**** Configuration and permissions

Use table-driven cases and a few realistic inherited Org configurations to establish:

- Existing file configurations still work.
- Resource types are checked.
- Web-only configuration works without a root.
- Project tools still require a root.
- Include/exclude composition remains resource-local with union across grants.
- Redefinition and clearing behave correctly.
- Inactive operation grants can authorize patch operations without exposing standalone tools.
- Missing underlying grants reject the whole patch.

**** File operations

Use temporary projects containing several files and clean/dirty visiting buffers.

Cover:

- Successful deletion and a mixed add/update/delete patch.
- No target changes when any operation is unauthorized, malformed, ambiguous, stale, or conflicts with a dirty buffer.
- Existing edit/create behavior remains intact.
- Encoding, line endings, modes, and buffer state are preserved as specified.
- Final symlinks, outside-root parent resolution, existing add targets, and aliasing cannot bypass validation.
- A concurrent source change is not overwritten.
- Failure partway through commit triggers rollback.
- Failed or unsafe rollback retains recovery data and reports the actual state.
- Trash behavior is exercised without touching the user’s real trash.

Inject failures at meaningful I/O boundaries. Assert filesystem and buffer outcomes, not internal helper-call sequences.

**** Web fetching

Use two layers:

1. Pure table-driven tests for URL matching, normalization, address classes, and redirect authorization.
2. Transport tests using a small local HTTP fixture with explicit local-service permission.

Do not access the public Internet in tests.

Exercise:

- Apex versus subdomains and ~www~.
- Scheme and port separation.
- Explicit IP literals and mapped IPv6.
- Denied initial URLs causing no DNS or connection attempt.
- Relative redirects and redirects to denied destinations.
- Actual address pinning while preserving the requested hostname.
- Unknown-length/chunked oversized responses.
- MIME handling, output truncation, timeout, cancellation, and cleanup.
- Absence of credential/proxy inheritance.

Mock resolver results and environmental failures at boundaries. Do not mock the entire feature and then merely assert that it returned the expected fixture.

Add a chat-loop integration test proving that fetched text containing Org/Ende-looking content remains literal tool content and does not create configuration or alter permissions.

Replace brittle assertions such as “exactly nine tools are registered” with checks for required names, uniqueness, schema validity, and activation filtering.

#+begin_src markdown
Implement web_fetch, delete_file, and apply_patch in Ende.

Complete the implementation, tests, documentation, and integration in one working session. Do not stop after scaffolding or implementing only one tool.

Write elegant, idiomatic, readable, and efficient Emacs Lisp. Prefer small, cohesive functions and explicit data structures. Avoid speculative abstractions, unnecessary dependencies, unrelated refactoring, and compatibility aliases nobody needs.

Tests should establish useful behavioral guarantees, not mirror implementation details. Use representative scenarios and table-driven boundary tests rather than dozens of narrowly tailored tests.

Commit coherent, tested steps as you go. Commit subjects must be short, on-point, and human-readable. Do not commit unrelated working-tree changes, generated files, or temporary artifacts.

1. Inspect the repository and establish a baseline

   Before editing:

   - Read repository instructions and relevant documentation.
   - Inspect the working tree and preserve existing user changes.
   - Run make check and record any pre-existing failures.
   - Inspect the implementation and callers of:
     - ende-tool-register and ende-tool-call.
     - ende-tools--path-authorized-p.
     - ende-change-set and existing create/edit handlers.
     - Resource parsing, inheritance, and grants in ende-chat-config.el.
     - ende-project-path and visiting-buffer discovery.
     - Tool dispatch and cancellation in ende-chat.el and ende-queue.el.
     - Curl process management in ende-curl.el.
     - The dependency order in ende-reload.

   Relevant existing tests include:

   - tests/ende-tools-test.el
   - tests/ende-chat-config-test.el
   - tests/ende-chat-tools-test.el
   - tests/ende-transport-test.el

   Preserve these existing behaviors:

   - Untyped resources describe project-relative file patterns.
   - Configuration is parsed as data, never evaluated.
   - Resource and group redefinitions revoke inherited grants according to the existing rules.
   - Tool activation and resource assignment are separate.
   - Existing file tools operate on disk, not visiting-buffer contents.
   - edit_file currently permits dirty visiting buffers, leaves them untouched, and returns warnings. Existing tests explicitly cover this.

   Do not silently change that last behavior while sharing implementation with the new tools. Deletion and patching will use a stricter dirty-buffer policy.

2. Add typed resources and separate project-root requirements

   Suggested commit: Add typed tool resources

   Registry and dispatch:

   Represent these requirements independently:

   - Resource kind: file, url, or none.
   - Whether a project root is required.
   - Whether the tool mutates local files.

   Classify tools as follows:

   - Existing file readers: file resource, root required, no local mutation.
   - Existing create/edit tools: file resource, root required, local mutation.
   - delete_file and apply_patch: file resource, root required, local mutation.
   - project_info: no resource, root required, no local mutation.
   - web_fetch: url resource, no root required, no local mutation.

   “No local mutation” does not mean fetching is side-effect-free: it sends a network request.

   Keep existing registration calls working. Update consumers of the current requires-resource flag consistently. Avoid maintaining contradictory sources of truth.

   Resource syntax:

   Support the following forms:

       (resource source
         (include "src/**")
         (exclude "src/generated/**"))

       (resource source-explicit :type file
         (include "src/**"))

       (resource documentation :type url
         (include "https://example.com/**")
         (include "https://*.example.com/**")
         (exclude "https://private.example.com/**"))

   Requirements:

   - Omitted :type means file.
   - Preserve existing file-pattern behavior exactly. Do not reinterpret existing patterns as gitignore-style patterns.
   - Reject unsupported resource types, malformed options, and incompatible tool/resource assignments.
   - Preserve existing assignments to resource-free tools within file-tool groups.
   - Validate assignments to inactive tools too: those grants can authorize operations inside a patch.
   - Every resource still requires an explicit include.
   - Preserve inheritance, replacement/addition of grants, and grant revocation on resource redefinition.

   Resource composition remains:

   - Within a resource: at least one include matches and no exclude matches.
   - Across assigned resources: permission is the union of those resources.

   An exclusion in one resource does not override an allowance in another. Document this. Do not introduce a new global deny-list language.

   Root handling:

   - Require an explicit configured root only when an active tool requires one.
   - A web-only chat must work without a project, including when the current buffer is outside any project.
   - Avoid unconditional project discovery or root normalization in dispatch.
   - An explicitly supplied invalid root must still produce an error.
   - Keep model-originated calls bound to the frozen chat policy.
   - Preserve existing trusted, no-policy Elisp use of file tools.
   - Require an explicit URL policy for web_fetch. There must be no unrestricted-fetch fallback.

   URL policy:

   Put URL parsing and policy matching in a small module, such as ende-url.el, shared by configuration and fetching. Do not reuse the file wildcard matcher against complete URLs.

   Implement these fixed semantics:

   - Bare DNS hostnames are shorthand for HTTPS and all paths:
     - example.com means https://example.com/**
     - *.example.com means https://*.example.com/**
   - Patterns containing paths or ports must be absolute HTTP(S) patterns.
   - Exact hosts match exactly. Never automatically add or remove www.
   - *.example.com matches one or more subdomain labels, but not the apex or evil-example.com.
   - A whole-host * may match any DNS hostname. IP literals still require explicit literal-host rules.
   - HTTP and HTTPS are separate permissions.
   - An omitted port means the scheme’s default port.
   - Path matching is case-sensitive. * does not cross a slash; ** can.
   - Queries are allowed in request URLs but are not filtered by resource patterns.
   - Reject query/fragment syntax in patterns.
   - Strip request fragments before authorization and transmission.
   - Support exact IPv4 and bracketed IPv6 hosts.
   - No IP globs or CIDR syntax in this version.

   Use one canonicalization path for both policy patterns and requests:

   - Normalize hostname case, a terminal DNS dot, default ports, and path dot segments consistently.
   - Support ASCII/punycode hostnames initially. Reject raw Unicode hostnames with an actionable message.
   - Reject user information, control characters, backslashes, malformed escapes, legacy numeric IPv4 spellings, and IPv6 zone identifiers.
   - Normalize unreserved percent escapes before path matching.
   - Reject encoded path separators and nested escaping rather than guessing how another parser will interpret them.

   Authorize the normalized URL that will actually be transmitted. Document that path filters constrain outgoing URLs, not how a remote application routes or interprets them.

   Add policy and inheritance tests in this commit, including a rootless URL-tool fixture without exposing an unfinished production tool.

3. Share file mutation preparation and commit logic

   Suggested commit: Separate file preparation from writes

   Build a small internal mutation pipeline around the existing change-set machinery. Keep it in ende-tools.el unless a separate cohesive module is clearly warranted. Do not build a general transaction framework.

   Separate:

   - Resolving and authorizing an operation.
   - Reading its source and preparing its result.
   - Validating the complete batch.
   - Committing changes.
   - Recovering from a failed commit.

   Refactor existing create_file and edit_file to use the shared pieces without changing their schemas, normal behavior, or result fields.

   Preparation:

   - Enumerate all operations and paths first.
   - Authorize all operations before reading target contents or staging changes.
   - Detect duplicate canonical targets and conflicting operations on the same existing file.
   - Record original contents, coding system, line-ending style, mode, identity, and relevant preconditions.
   - Prepare all resulting contents before changing any target.
   - Require existing parent directories, matching current create_file behavior.
   - Apply reasonable limits to patch size, operation count, and cumulative prepared data.

   Keep public digest semantics compatible with read_file and file_info. For internal race detection and recovery, retain enough raw-byte and file-identity information to detect changes that a decoded-text digest would miss.

   Commit and recovery:

   Immediately before committing, recheck the complete batch. Recheck each target again before its individual mutation.

   - Revalidate containment, permissions, existence/nonexistence, identity, contents, and applicable dirty-buffer rules.
   - Use exclusive creation for new files. Never overwrite a file that appeared after preparation.
   - Preserve existing coding systems, line endings, and file modes.
   - Keep private recovery copies until the batch has completed.
   - Use restrictive permissions for temporary and recovery data.
   - Prevent overlapping Ende commits from interleaving on the same project.
   - Clean up owned resources with unwind-protect.

   If a commit fails:

   - Attempt rollback in reverse order.
   - Do not overwrite a concurrent user change while rolling back.
   - Retain recovery material when automatic restoration is unsafe or unsuccessful.
   - Return an honest per-file account of what was applied, restored, or still needs recovery.

   Do not claim crash-safe, multi-file atomicity or a race-free filesystem sandbox. Portable file operations do not provide those guarantees.

   The contract is:

   - A preflight permission, syntax, context, or precondition failure leaves every target file and visiting buffer unchanged.
   - A failure after committing starts triggers best-effort rollback and an explicit recovery report.

   Extend the dispatcher narrowly so structured mutation failures retain their details. Preserve the existing readable :error string and use :ok nil for failed or partial batches. Do not let the generic error handler discard the recovery report.

   Never modify, save, revert, or kill visiting buffers as part of these operations.

4. Implement file deletion

   Suggested commit: Add safe file deletion

   Register delete_file with:

   - path: required project-relative file path.
   - expected_digest: optional digest precondition.

   Contract:

   - Delete one existing regular file.
   - No directories, recursive deletion, or wildcard expansion.
   - Require the delete_file resource grant independently of read/edit permissions.
   - Reject final-component symlinks, including dangling symlinks.
   - Resolve parent directories and enforce the existing project boundary and global exclusions.
   - Reject absolute paths, remote paths, invalid path characters, and parent-traversal components in this new API.
   - Refuse deletion if any visiting buffer for the target has unsaved changes.
   - Leave clean visiting buffers intact and return a warning that saving them could recreate the file.
   - When supplied, expected_digest must match the digest exposed by existing file-read/info tools.
   - Missing files and stale digests are errors, not silent successes.

   Check the final component for symlinks before calling code that resolves it through file-truename. Otherwise, deletion could remove the link’s target.

   Honor delete-by-moving-to-trash explicitly. Never silently fall back to permanent deletion when a requested trash operation fails.

   Use the shared mutation pipeline. Capture visiting-buffer information before the file disappears.

   Return a normal JSON-compatible result identifying the operation, project-relative path, status, and relevant buffer warnings. Do not return deleted contents.

   There is no model-controlled force option.

5. Implement multi-file patching

   Suggested commit: Add multi-file patches

   Register apply_patch with a required patch string.

   Use a documented, deliberately limited Codex-style text format. Put pure parsing and hunk application in ende-patch.el. Keep filesystem access and authorization out of the parser.

   Supported format:

   - "*** Begin Patch"
   - "*** Add File: PATH"
   - "*** Update File: PATH"
   - "*** Delete File: PATH"
   - Bare "@@" hunk separators.
   - Context lines beginning with a space.
   - Removed lines beginning with "-".
   - Added lines beginning with "+".
   - "*** End of File" to constrain an update hunk to EOF.
   - "*** End Patch"

   Parser and matching requirements:

   - Parse the entire input before performing filesystem work.
   - Require project-relative literal paths.
   - Match context exactly and case-sensitively.
   - Reject absent, ambiguous, overlapping, or out-of-order hunk matches.
   - Require context for insertion-only hunks, except when updating an empty file.
   - Reject multiple operation records targeting the same canonical file.
   - Reject malformed input and unsupported constructs explicitly.

   No fuzzy matching, shell invocation, external patch command, rename/move syntax, binary patches, mode changes, symlink creation, or implicit directory creation in this version.

   Treat the protocol as logical lines:

   - Preserve existing encoding, line endings, and final-newline style on updates.
   - New files use UTF-8 with Unix line endings.
   - Nonempty added files end in a newline.
   - Empty additions are supported.

   Document this as a supported subset, not full compatibility with another tool’s patch language.

   Authorization:

   Every operation must pass both:

   - The apply_patch resource scope.
   - The corresponding underlying operation scope:
     - Update requires edit_file.
     - Add requires create_file.
     - Delete requires delete_file.

   Underlying standalone tools need not be active or exposed to the model. Their grants must exist and authorize the operation.

   Use a shared authorization helper with an explicit operation/tool identity. Do not invoke ende-tool-call recursively or temporarily weaken the policy.

   Document and test these consequences:

   - A patch cannot delete merely because it can edit.
   - Hiding delete_file does not revoke an existing delete grant.
   - clear-tools controls exposure; grant removal controls operation authorization.

   Batch behavior:

   - Run the entire patch through the shared preparation/commit pipeline.
   - Do not loop over immediately mutating tool handlers.
   - Reject final-component symlinks.
   - Refuse the entire patch if any affected visiting buffer is dirty.
   - Apply the same deletion/trash rules as standalone deletion.
   - Reject adding over any existing filesystem entry.
   - Leave visiting buffers untouched and report stale-buffer warnings.
   - Return per-file operation results as a real JSON array.
   - Report recovery details on commit failure.

6. Implement bounded, permission-aware web fetching

   Suggested commit: Add bounded web fetching

   Register web_fetch with:

   - url: required absolute HTTP(S) URL.
   - max_chars: optional lower output limit.

   Keep transport code in ende-web-fetch.el, separate from provider authentication. Reuse neutral process/cleanup helpers where appropriate, but do not route fetching through provider-profile validation or credential construction.

   Destination authorization:

   For the initial URL and every redirect:

   - Parse and normalize the URL.
   - Check its URL-resource permission before DNS lookup.
   - Resolve the destination.
   - Classify the returned addresses.
   - Connect only to a vetted address.

   Use numeric address parsing and prefix checks, not textual prefix matching.

   By default reject non-public destinations, including loopback, private, link-local, unspecified, multicast, reserved, and other special-use ranges.

   Handle IPv4-mapped IPv6 explicitly. Unsupported transition/address forms must fail closed rather than bypass classification.

   Use a clearly documented address-classification table. Do not download policy data at runtime.

   For local services, support this explicit resource option:

       (resource local-service :type url :allow-private-addresses t
         (include "http://127.0.0.1:3000/**"))

   Limit this opt-in to loopback, RFC1918 IPv4, and IPv6 unique-local addresses. Link-local metadata endpoints and other special-use destinations remain blocked.

   Private-enabled resources must use exact hosts, not wildcard hosts. The exception applies only when that resource actually permits the requested URL. It must not become a global switch.

   Require all resolved addresses to satisfy the applicable policy before selecting one.

   Pin the connection to the selected address while retaining the original hostname for HTTP Host, TLS SNI, and certificate verification. Curl’s --resolve is suitable for this.

   Do not validate DNS and then let curl independently resolve the hostname again. Never fall back to an unvetted address.

   Transport restrictions:

   Use curl through argument vectors, never through a shell.

   - Disable curl configuration-file loading.
   - Disable ambient proxies for this tool, regardless of the provider proxy option.
   - Do not inherit provider headers, auth-source credentials, cookies, netrc configuration, or other authenticated client state.
   - Keep TLS certificate verification enabled.
   - Allow HTTP and HTTPS only.
   - Disable curl URL globbing.
   - Do not use automatic redirect following.
   - Follow only supported HTTP redirects, resolving relative locations and reauthorizing each hop.
   - Do not fetch scripts, stylesheets, images, or other subresources.

   The model cannot supply methods, headers, credentials, proxy settings, or transport flags.

   Use finite tool-specific defaults:

   - 10-second connection deadline.
   - 30-second overall call deadline.
   - Five redirects.
   - 2 MiB cumulative response-body limit.
   - 64 KiB cumulative response-header limit.
   - 50,000 returned characters.

   The overall deadline must cover resolution and redirect processing too. Do not assume a timer can interrupt a blocking resolver primitive; use a bounded resolver execution path.

   Enforce byte limits during receipt, including responses without Content-Length and chunked responses. A post-download size check or curl’s filesize option alone is insufficient.

   Request identity content encoding and reject unsupported encodings in this version rather than adding an unbounded decompression path.

   A model-supplied max_chars may lower the configured output ceiling, never raise it.

   Content and result behavior:

   - Return bounded raw text/HTML.
   - Do not introduce browser rendering or HTML extraction.
   - Accept documented textual MIME types, including HTML, plain text, JSON, and XML.
   - Reject binary or unsupported content types clearly.

   Successful results must include:

   - Requested URL.
   - Final URL.
   - HTTP status.
   - Content type.
   - Content.
   - Whether the returned text was truncated.

   A completed textual HTTP error response can be returned with its HTTP status.

   DNS, TLS, transport, policy, timeout, and hard-size failures use :ok nil.

   Only deliberate output clipping sets truncated. Do not disguise an interrupted transfer as a successful truncated response.

   Fetched content remains untrusted tool data. Never evaluate it, activate embedded Ende configuration, or let it alter the frozen policy.

   Cancellation and integration:

   Preserve the ordinary synchronous ende-tool-call result interface. Internally, use managed processes and a cancellable wait rather than an uninterruptible process call.

   Connect chat cancellation to the active fetch. On cancellation:

   - Stop resolver and HTTP processes.
   - Release owned buffers, files, and timers.
   - Do not execute later tool calls in that round.
   - Do not start another model request.

   Keep this integration small. Do not redesign the whole scheduler.

   Update module loading and ende-reload in dependency order. Avoid require cycles between tools, configuration, URL policy, and transport modules.

7. Test behavior, not implementation shape

   Web fetching:

   Use two layers:

   - Pure table-driven tests for URL matching, normalization, address classes, and redirect authorization.
